Reproducing Ray, Moaddab & McDannald (2022) from CRCNS nac-1¶

Paper. Ray MH, Moaddab M, McDannald MA (2022). Threat and bidirectional valence signaling in the nucleus accumbens core. J Neurosci 42(5):817-833. doi:10.1523/JNEUROSCI.1107-21.2021. The claims file was written from the version of record (Introduction, Methods, Results, legends and Discussion); the Extended Data figures were not available.

Data set. CRCNS nac-1, doi:10.6080/K0M906VB, read from the public bucket at https://crcnsarchive.s3-us-west-2.amazonaws.com/nac-1/live/data.zip, streamed with range requests. CRCNS data sets are not versioned, so the version is recorded as unversioned, and the file read is pinned by its MD5 in the data set's checksums.md5: data.zip ab13407080360d7d683ef818ae66f33a (33,243,283 bytes, last modified 2026-02-11). There is no other version to choose from: the bucket holds only live/ for nac-1 (no old/), and the data description is version 0.6 (Feb 9, 2022), its initial version.

What the data set holds. One MATLAB file per unit for the 368 units (spike times, mean waveform, and the session's event channels: cues, shocks, pellets, nose pokes), a precomputed cube.mat that the authors' script builds from those files, and the authors' MATLAB scripts (cubeScriptPR25.m and NAccAnalysis/*.m). It holds no pre-surgery behavior sessions and no population (cluster) labels.

The authors' scripts. The paper links no analysis code (its lab page returns 404). The scripts in the data set are the authors' code, and this notebook takes from them the details the Methods and the claims file leave open, citing them. They give the event channel map, which the data description lacks.

Every random draw uses a generator seeded for its own claim section.

Claim Data Status
units-recorded data set tested
cue-responsive-193 data set tested
fig1c-acquisition data set blocked: the pre-surgery sessions are not in the data set
fig1g-discrimination, fig1h-poke-rate-unrelated, fig1h-discrimination-safety data set tested
fig2-five-clusters, populations-by-rat, most-units-threat data set tested
fig3ab-rate-cv-unimodal, fig3c-waveform-bimodal data set tested

fig6-similarity-anova, fig6d, fig6e, fig6f, fig6-thi-greatest-similarity data set tested
fig7-tuning-curves, fig8ab, fig8cd, fig8ef data set tested
fig9a, fig9bcd, fig9efg, fig9hij (reward) data set tested

import collections
import time
import warnings

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd
from scipy import stats

import nac

pd.set_option("display.width", 220)
pd.set_option("display.max_columns", 40)
pd.set_option("display.max_rows", 300)
warnings.filterwarnings("ignore", category=RuntimeWarning)

started = time.time()
U = nac.load_units()
CUBE = nac.load_cube()
print(f"loaded {len(U)} unit files from {nac.URL} in {time.time() - started:.0f} s")

loaded 368 unit files from https://crcnsarchive.s3-us-west-2.amazonaws.com/nac-1/live/data.zip in 56 s

Data check¶

The loader assigns the cue channels to danger, uncertainty and safety by the program letter in the file name, as cubeScriptPR25.m does, and splits uncertainty trials into shock and omission by a shock within 30 s of onset. The first check compares what the loader computes from the raw files with the authors' cube.mat: trial onsets and types, the binned firing, and the suppression ratios.

In [2]:
cube_names = ["".join(CUBE.name[0][j][i] for j in range(22)) for i in range(len(CUBE.name[0][0]))]
cube_index = {n: i for i, n in enumerate(cube_names)}
raw250 = CUBE.fire[0, 0].raw[0, 0].ms250[0, 0].interval
cer = CUBE.cer[0, 0].cue
n_tag = n_raw = n_sr = 0
for u in U:
    i = cube_index[u["filename"] + u["neuron"]]
    n_tag += np.allclose(CUBE.tag[:, 1, i], u["onsets"]) and np.array_equal(CUBE.tag[:, 0, i], nac.TRIAL_TYPES + 1)
    n_raw += np.array_equal(np.floor(nac.binned_rates(u["spikes"], u["onsets"])), raw250[:, :, i].astype(float))
    n_sr += np.allclose(nac.suppression(u, "one")[0], cer[:, 0, i])
print(f"units in cube.mat: {len(cube_names)}; matched by name: {sum(u['filename'] + u['neuron'] in cube_index for u in U)}")
print(f"trial onsets and types equal to cube.tag: {n_tag} of {len(U)}")
print(f"floored 250-ms rates equal to cube.fire.raw.ms250: {n_raw} of {len(U)}")
print(f"suppression ratios (0/0 set to 1, as the script does) equal to cube.cer.cue: {n_sr} of {len(U)}")
print()
print("trials per type (danger, unc shock, unc omission, safety):", collections.Counter(u["n_type"] for u in U))
print("event channels:", collections.Counter(tuple(u["channels"]) for u in U))
print("programs:", dict(collections.Counter(u["program"] for u in U)))

for u in {u["session"]: u for u in U}.values():
    for t in u["onsets"][nac.TRIAL_TYPES <= 1]:
        d = u["shock"][(u["shock"] > t)] - t
        sh.append(d.min() if len(d) else np.nan)
sh = np.array(sh)
print(f"shock after onset on danger and uncertainty-shock trials: {np.nanmin(sh):.2f} to {np.nanmax(sh):.2f} s (paper: 2 s after the 10-s cue, so 12 s)")
print(f"shocks per session: {collections.Counter(len(u['shock']) for u in {u['session']: u for u in U}.values())} (design: 6; EVT01)")
pb = CUBE.poke[0, 0].base
m20 = m2 = 0
for u in U:

    m20 += np.array_equal(nac.count_in(u["poke"], u["onsets"] - 20, u["onsets"]) * 3, pb[:, 0, i].astype(int))
    m2 += np.array_equal(nac.count_in(u["poke"], u["onsets"] - 2, u["onsets"]) * 30, pb[:, 0, i].astype(int))
print(f"cube.poke.base equals the 20-s pre-cue poke rate (pokes/min) in {m20} units, the 2-s rate in {m2} (description: '2 s prior')")
w = np.array([u["wave"] for u in U])

What the checks show. The loader reproduces the authors' cube exactly (trial onsets and types, binned firing, suppression ratios) for all 368 units, so the event channel map taken from the script is the one the authors used. Every session has 4, 2, 6 and 4 trials of the four types and 6 shocks, 12 s after onset, as the Methods describe. Onsets are 203 to 265 s apart (median 233 s), consistent with a mean ITI of 3.5 min plus the trial. The data description says the cube's poke.base is the rate 2 s before the cue; the values are the 20-s rate (a description error, recorded as a finding).

Coverage. The files hold spikes, pokes and pellets only from 20 s before each onset to about 33 s after it: the recording is paused between trials. The data description does not say so.

Duplicates and clocks. A recording deposited twice would show identical spike trains; units of one session must share one set of event times. The clock check uses contrasts that only hold if the event times and the spikes and pokes share a clock: the suppression of poking to danger against safety, and the fraction of units whose firing changes at cue onset, each at the true onsets and at onsets moved 10 s earlier. The shifted onsets must fall in recorded time: 10 s earlier is the only shift that keeps a 10-s baseline and a 10-s pseudo-cue inside the recorded segment and away from the cue.

sig = collections.defaultdict(list)
for u in U:
    sig[(len(u["spikes"]), round(float(u["spikes"].sum()), 4))].append(u["file"])
print("units with identical spike trains:", [v for v in sig.values() if len(v) > 1])
by_session = collections.defaultdict(list)
for u in U:
    by_session[u["session"]].append(u)
print("sessions whose units disagree on event times:",
      [s for s, v in by_session.items() if len({(round(float(x["onsets"].sum()), 4), len(x["poke"]), len(x["pellet"])) for x in v}) > 1])
ev_sets = collections.defaultdict(set)
for s, v in by_session.items():
    ev_sets[(round(float(v[0]["onsets"].sum()), 4), len(v[0]["poke"]))].add(s)
print("event sets shared by two sessions:", [x for x in ev_sets.values() if len(x) > 1])
same_name = collections.Counter((u["session"], u["neuron"]) for u in U)
print("session and unit name used twice:", [k for k, c in same_name.items() if c > 1])

cover = []
for u in U:
    on = np.sort(u["onsets"])
    for name in ("spikes", "poke", "pellet"):
        x = u[name]
        if len(x):
            rel = x - on[np.clip(np.searchsorted(on, x + 20, side="right") - 1, 0, 15)]
            cover.append((name, rel.min(), rel.max()))
cover = pd.DataFrame(cover, columns=["stream", "min_rel", "max_rel"]).groupby("stream").agg(["min", "max"])
print("time of every spike, poke and pellet relative to the trial onset before it (s):")
print(cover.round(2))
gaps = np.concatenate([np.diff(u["spikes"]) for u in U])
print(f"inter-spike intervals longer than 100 s: {np.sum(gaps > 100)} (the recording is paused between trials, EVT14)")

def shifted(u, dt):
    v = dict(u)
    v["onsets"] = u["onsets"] + dt
    return v

sr = []
for v in by_session.values():
    s, _ = nac.suppression(v[0], "drop")
    sr.append([np.nanmean(s[nac.CUE_OF_TRIAL == 0]), np.nanmean(s[nac.CUE_OF_TRIAL == 2])])
sr = np.array(sr)
print(f"suppression ratio at the true onsets: danger {np.nanmean(sr[:, 0]):.3f}, safety {np.nanmean(sr[:, 1]):.3f}, "
      f"sessions with danger above safety {np.mean(sr[:, 0] > sr[:, 1]):.3f}")
# a pseudo-trial must lie inside the recorded segment and away from the cue: onsets 10 s earlier
# give a 10-s baseline 20 to 10 s before and a 'cue' in the 10 s before the true onset
pk = []
for v in by_session.values():
    u = v[0]
    for dt in (0, -10):
        on = u["onsets"] + dt
        b = nac.count_in(u["poke"], on - 10, on) / 10
        c = nac.count_in(u["poke"], on, on + 10) / 10
        pk.append(dict(shift_s=dt, danger_cue_minus_base=np.mean((c - b)[nac.CUE_OF_TRIAL == 0]),
                       safety_cue_minus_base=np.mean((c - b)[nac.CUE_OF_TRIAL == 2])))
pk = pd.DataFrame(pk).groupby("shift_s").mean()
pk["units_with_cue_response"] = [np.mean([nac.screen(shifted(u, dt), "pooled")[0] for u in U]) for dt in pk.index]
print("poke rate (per s) in the 10-s cue minus the 10 s before, mean over sessions, and the share of units passing")
print("screen reading (a), at the true onsets and 10 s earlier:")
print(pk.round(3).to_string())

def show_lines(path, ranges):
    lines = z_scripts.read(path).decode("latin-1").splitlines()
    print(f"--- {path}")
    for a, b in ranges:
        for n in range(a, b + 1):
            print(f"{n:5d}  {lines[n - 1]}")
        print("  ...")

units-recorded¶

368 NAcc single units were recorded from seven rats over 95 fear discrimination sessions.

A session is a rat and a date (the file name acRRMMDDYY...); each date has one behavior program per rat. The authors' mainAnalysis.m counts sessions as distinct rows of suppression ratios, which is shown as a variant.

In [6]:
n_units = len(U)
n_sessions = len({u["session"] for u in U})
n_rats = len({u["rat"] for u in U})
n_files = len({u["filename"] for u in U})
sr_rows = len({tuple(np.round(nac.suppression(u, "one")[0], 8)) for u in U})
print(f"units {n_units} (paper 368), sessions {n_sessions} (paper 95), rats {n_rats} (paper 7)")
print(f"distinct rat, date and program: {n_files}; distinct rows of 16 suppression ratios (mainAnalysis.m's count): {sr_rows}")
print("sessions per rat:", dict(sorted(collections.Counter(s[:4] for s in {u["session"] for u in U}).items())))

units 368 (paper 368), sessions 90 (paper 95), rats 7 (paper 7)
distinct rat, date and program: 90; distinct rows of 16 suppression ratios (mainAnalysis.m's count): 84
sessions per rat: {'ac01': 17, 'ac03': 17, 'ac04': 10, 'ac05': 17, 'ac06': 16, 'ac07': 7, 'ac08': 6}

Result: not reproduced. The data set holds 368 units from 7 rats, as the paper says, but from 90 distinct sessions (rat and date), not 95; counting distinct rows of suppression ratios, as the authors' script does, gives 84. The criterion's tolerance is zero for each count and it names no partial outcome, so the session count decides it.

cue-responsive-193¶

The cue-responsive screen identified 193 units (about 53% of all units) from 82 sessions, with at least seven cue-responsive units in each rat.

Readings of the criterion: (a) the eight uncertainty trials tested as one cue; (b) shock and omission trials as two cues. Rates in Hz over the 10-s baseline and the first 1 s and last 5 s of each cue, paired two-tailed t-tests at p < 0.05. The authors' cubeScriptPR25.m floors each rate to an integer before mainAnalysis.m runs the same paired tests (ttest, pooled uncertainty rows 5 to 12); that is shown as a variant. A test with no variance in the differences gives no p-value and does not count, as MATLAB's ttest returns NaN.

In [7]:
screens = {}
for label, unc, fl in (("(a) uncertainty pooled", "pooled", False), ("(b) shock and omission split", "split", False),
                       ("script: pooled, floored rates", "pooled", True), ("script floor, split", "split", True)):
    screens[label] = np.array([nac.screen(u, unc, fl)[0] for u in U])
rows = []
for label, r in screens.items():
    per_rat = collections.Counter(u["rat"] for u, x in zip(U, r) if x)
    rows.append(dict(reading=label, responsive=int(r.sum()), share=r.mean(), sessions=len({u["session"] for u, x in zip(U, r) if x}),
                     min_per_rat=min(per_rat.values()), per_rat=dict(sorted(per_rat.items())),
                     within_tolerance=abs(r.sum() - 193) <= 19.3 and abs(len({u["session"] for u, x in zip(U, r) if x}) - 82) <= 8.2 and min(per_rat.values()) >= 7))
print(pd.DataFrame(rows).round(3).to_string(index=False))
print("expected: 193 +- 19.3 units, 82 +- 8.2 sessions, at least 7 per rat")
print("null reference: onsets moved 10 s earlier, so that the baseline is 20 to 10 s and the 'cue' the 10 s")
print("before the real onset, all inside the recorded segment and before any cue:")
for label, unc, fl in (("(a) uncertainty pooled", "pooled", False), ("(b) shock and omission split", "split", False),
                       ("script: pooled, floored rates", "pooled", True)):
    fp = np.array([nac.screen(shifted(u, -10), unc, fl)[0] for u in U])
    print(f"  {label}: {fp.sum()} of {len(fp)} units pass ({fp.mean():.3f}); at the true onsets {screens[label].mean():.3f}")

  script: pooled, floored rates: 79 of 368 units pass (0.215); at the true onsets 0.524

Result: reproduced. Under reading (b) the screen finds 211 units from 81 sessions, with at least 7 per rat, all within tolerance; under reading (a) it finds 219 units, above the tolerance of 193 +- 19.3, from 82 sessions. The authors' own procedure (floored rates, pooled uncertainty) finds exactly 193 units from 82 sessions, with at least 7 per rat.

With onsets moved 10 s earlier, inside the recorded segment and before any cue, 25.8% of units pass reading (a), 29.1% reading (b) and 21.5% the script's screen, against 59.5%, 57.3% and 52.4% at the true onsets; six uncorrected tests at 0.05 would pass about 26% of units by chance. The paper states no chance level.

The later claims need the paper's 193 units. They use the set of the authors' procedure (mainAnalysis.m: rates floored to whole Hz as cubeScriptPR25.m stores them, uncertainty pooled), which gives the paper's 193 units and 82 sessions exactly. This is an assumption: it is neither reading of the claims file, and the Methods say "mean firing rate (Hz)" and do not mention flooring. The units of reading (b) (211) give other populations (shown under fig2-five-clusters for reading (a)).

Behavior per rat (fig1g, fig1h)¶

Per session, the mean suppression ratio of each cue over its trials (uncertainty pooled), and the mean baseline poke rate (pokes per min, 20 s before onset). Per rat, the mean over the chosen sessions (the authors' mainAnalysis.m averages the session rows of each rat). Session readings of fig1g: (a) the 82 sessions with a cue-responsive unit; (b) all sessions (90 here); (c) the sessions of the three main populations (computed after the clustering, below). Readings of a 0/0 suppression ratio from the claims file: left out, or 0; the authors' scripts set it to 1 (variant).

In [10]:
def behavior(sessions, nan_reading):
    per_rat = collections.defaultdict(list)
    for s in sorted(sessions):
        u = by_session[s][0]
        sr, base = nac.suppression(u, nan_reading)
        per_rat[u["rat"]].append([np.nanmean(sr[nac.CUE_OF_TRIAL == k]) for k in range(3)] + [base.mean()])
    rats = sorted(per_rat)
    return pd.DataFrame([np.mean(per_rat[r], 0) for r in rats], index=rats,
                        columns=["danger", "uncertainty", "safety", "baseline_pokes_per_min"])

def fig1g(beh, rng, mirrored=None):
    F = nac.rm_anova1(beh[["danger", "uncertainty", "safety"]].to_numpy())
    d = {"D-U": beh.danger - beh.uncertainty, "U-S": beh.uncertainty - beh.safety, "D-S": beh.danger - beh.safety}
    out = dict(F=F[0], df=f"{F[1]},{F[2]}", p=F[3])
    for k, x in d.items():
        m, lo, hi = nac.boot_ci(x.to_numpy(), rng, mirrored=mirrored)
        out[k] = m
        out[k + " CI"] = f"[{lo:.2f}, {hi:.2f}]"
        out[k + " excl0"] = lo > 0 or hi < 0
    return out

def fig1g_seeds(beh, seeds=range(1001, 1051), mirrored=None):
    """Median CI bounds over 50 seeds: with seven rats a bound near zero moves with the seed."""
    out = {}
    d = {"D-U": beh.danger - beh.uncertainty, "U-S": beh.uncertainty - beh.safety, "D-S": beh.danger - beh.safety}
    for k, x in d.items():
        b = np.array([nac.boot_ci(x.to_numpy(), np.random.default_rng(s), mirrored=mirrored)[1:] for s in seeds])
        lo, hi = np.median(b, 0)
        out[k + " median CI"] = f"[{lo:.3f}, {hi:.3f}]"
        out[k + " excl0 (median)"] = lo > 0 or hi < 0
        out[k + " excl0 share"] = np.mean((b[:, 0] > 0) | (b[:, 1] < 0))
    return out

session_sets = {"(a) 82 with a cue-responsive unit": {u["session"] for u in R}, "(b) all": set(by_session)}
nan_readings = {"drop": "drop", "0": "zero", "1 (script)": "one"}

fig1g-discrimination¶

During the recording sessions all rats discriminated the cues, with suppression ratios highest to danger, intermediate to uncertainty and lowest to safety; every cue pair differed.

  seed 2: D-U [0.14, 0.27], U-S [-0.13, 0.59], D-S [0.15, 0.86]

Result: reproduced, under one reading (all sessions, 0/0 read as 0), with a CI bound near zero. The cue effect holds under every reading (F(2,12) from 52.6 to 57.4, p < 1e-5), and the three mean differences are within tolerance under every reading (0.20 to 0.22, 0.47 to 0.48, 0.67 to 0.70). The danger minus uncertainty and danger minus safety CIs exclude zero under every reading. With the standard interval the uncertainty minus safety CI excludes zero only with all sessions and 0/0 read as 0 (median bounds over 50 seeds [0.012, 0.599], excluding zero in 68% of seeds; [0.04, 0.59] with seed 1001); under the other readings its lower bound is below zero, because one rat (ac01) has a difference of 0.15. In the CI section that reading reproduces in 3 of 5 seeds with the analytic SE and 2 of 5 with the inner bootstrap SE. With the authors' handling of 0/0 (set to 1) and the 82 sessions, F(2,12) = 57.41 and the means 0.21, 0.47 and 0.69 equal the paper's values exactly. The paper's CIs are not standard studentized intervals of these data: they match, within resampling noise, the bootstrap-t quantiles added to the mean without reflection ([0.36, 1.03] against the paper's [0.35, 1.03] for uncertainty minus safety, [0.52, 1.18] against [0.50, 1.25] for danger minus safety). With that form every CI excludes zero under every reading and every seed (median uncertainty minus safety bounds 0.33 to 0.35 at the lower end), and the claim is reproduced under all six readings.

This outcome rests on an assumption. The criterion accepts the claim under one reading, and the reproducing reading (all sessions, 0/0 read as 0) is not the authors' handling. With their handling (0/0 set to 1), which fig1h-poke-rate-unrelated adopts because it gives the paper's F exactly, the uncertainty minus safety CI contains zero in every one of the 50 seeds with the standard interval, in both session sets (median lower bounds -0.125 and -0.084).

fig1h-poke-rate-unrelated and fig1h-discrimination-safety¶

Baseline nose poke rate was unrelated to suppression to any cue and to overall discrimination. Overall discrimination ... correlated negatively with the safety suppression ratio across rats.

In [12]:
rows = []
for sl, S in session_sets.items():
    for nl, nr in nan_readings.items():
        beh = behavior(S, nr)
        disc = beh.danger - beh.safety
        row = dict(sessions=sl, zero_over_zero=nl)
        for k, y in (("danger", beh.danger), ("uncertainty", beh.uncertainty), ("safety", beh.safety), ("D-S", disc)):
            r = stats.pearsonr(beh.baseline_pokes_per_min, y)
            row[f"poke~{k} R"] = r.statistic
            row[f"poke~{k} p"] = r.pvalue
        r = stats.pearsonr(disc, beh.safety)
        row["D-S~safety R"] = r.statistic
        row["D-S~safety p"] = r.pvalue
        rows.append(row)
fig1h_table = pd.DataFrame(rows)
print(fig1h_table.round(3).to_string(index=False))
print("p of discrimination (D-S) against safety, unrounded:")

Result (fig1h-poke-rate-unrelated): reproduced, resting on the 0/0 handling. With the authors' handling of 0/0 ratios (set to 1, which gives the paper's F exactly) or with them left out, no correlation of baseline poke rate has p < 0.05 in either session set (smallest p 0.26; largest |R| 0.49). With 0/0 read as 0, baseline poke rate correlates with danger suppression in the 82 sessions (R = 0.76, p = 0.048).

Result (fig1h-discrimination-safety): reproduced. R is -0.98 or -0.99 with p from 4.5e-5 to 1.4e-4 under every reading (the paper: R = -0.98, p = 1.41e-4).

fig2-five-clusters¶

K-means clustering of the 193 cue-responsive units on six firing epochs ... gave five functional populations; Threat Excited (5), Threat Inhibited (26), Danger Excited (55), Bidirectional Valence (91) and Non-Selective (16).

Matrix readings: mean normalized firing (onset, first 1 s; late, last 5 s; per cue), under the four normalization readings of the claims file (N1 to N4), or mean firing in Hz. Start readings: (i) the lowest summed distance over 100 k-means++ starts; (ii) the most frequent partition over 100 starts. Clusters are named by matching their centroids to the Results' patterns. The authors' script clusters the normalization of cubeScriptPR25.m with one start after rng(1) (mainAnalysis.m, "K means clustering"); that normalization is shown as a variant (the MATLAB random stream cannot be repeated here).

In [13]:
norm = {}
for k, kw in nac.NORM_READINGS.items():
    norm[k] = np.array([nac.norm_methods(u, **kw)[0] for u in R])
norm["script normalization"] = np.array([nac.norm_code(u)[0] for u in R])
mats = {k: np.array([nac.epochs(t) for t in v]) for k, v in norm.items()}
mats["Hz"] = np.array([np.concatenate([[nac.window_rate(u["spikes"], u["onsets"], a, b)[nac.CUE_OF_TRIAL == c].mean()
                                        for c in range(3)] for a, b in ((0, 1), (5, 10))]) for u in R])
print({k: v.shape for k, v in mats.items()})

{'N1 4 types, unc = mean of 8 trials': (193, 6), 'N2 4 types, unc = mean of 2 type traces': (193, 6), 'N3 3 types, unc = mean of 8 trials': (193, 6), 'N4 3 types, unc = mean of 2 type traces': (193, 6), 'script normalization': (193, 6), 'Hz': (193, 6)}
In [14]:
partitions = {}
rows = []
for k, X in mats.items():
    runs = nac.kmeans_runs(X, 5, 100, seed=2000)
    best = min(runs, key=lambda r: r[1])[0]
    freq_count = collections.Counter(nac.canonical(r[0]) for r in runs)
    freq, n_freq = freq_count.most_common(1)[0]
    for start, lab in (("(i) best of 100", np.asarray(best)), ("(ii) most frequent", np.asarray(freq))):
        names, cos = nac.name_clusters(X, lab)
        pops = np.array([names[l] for l in lab])
        key = f"{k} | {start}"
        partitions[key] = pops
        cnt = collections.Counter(pops)
        rows.append(dict(matrix=k, start=start, times_found=n_freq if start.startswith("(ii)") else
                         sum(nac.canonical(r[0]) == nac.canonical(best) for r in runs),
                         **{p: cnt[p] for p in nac.POPULATIONS}, min_cosine=min(cos.values()),
                         within_tolerance=all(abs(cnt[p] - e) <= e * 0.1 for p, e in zip(nac.POPULATIONS, (5, 26, 55, 91, 16)))))
fig2_table = pd.DataFrame(rows)
print(fig2_table.round(2).to_string(index=False))
print("paper: Threat Excited 5, Threat Inhibited 26, Danger Excited 55, Bidirectional Valence 91, Non-Selective 16 (10% tolerance)")

claims_rows = fig2_table[fig2_table.matrix.isin(list(nac.NORM_READINGS))]
best_row = claims_rows.loc[claims_rows.min_cosine.idxmax()]
PRIMARY = f"{best_row.matrix} | {best_row.start}"
PRIMARY_NORM = best_row.matrix
alt_row = claims_rows.drop(index=best_row.name).sort_values("min_cosine").iloc[-1]
ALT = f"{alt_row.matrix} | {alt_row.start}"
FITTING = [f"{r.matrix} | {r.start}" for r in fig2_table.itertuples() if r.min_cosine >= 0.7]
print(f"next best (alternative partition, shown with every population claim): {ALT}, min cosine {alt_row.min_cosine:.2f}")
print(f"partitions whose clusters fit their names (smallest cosine at least 0.7), used in the outcome tallies: {FITTING}")

def tally(t, label="outcomes"):
    """Outcome counts over the partitions whose clusters fit their names; partitions with a
    smaller cosine have clusters that do not show the named patterns, and their outcomes mean
    nothing. Also the outcomes on the primary and the alternative partition."""
    if "partition" not in t:
        print(f"\n{label}:", dict(collections.Counter(t.outcome)))
        return
    fit = t[t.partition.isin(FITTING)]
    print(f"\n{label}, fitting partitions:", dict(collections.Counter(fit.outcome)),
          "| primary:", dict(collections.Counter(t[t.partition == PRIMARY].outcome)),
          "| alternative:", dict(collections.Counter(t[t.partition == ALT].outcome)))
print(f"primary partition (claims-file readings, highest smallest cosine to the five patterns): {PRIMARY}, min cosine {best_row.min_cosine:.2f}")
print("centroids (danger, uncertainty, safety onset; then late cue) of each partition:")
for key, pops in partitions.items():
    X = mats[key.split(" | ")[0]]
    print(key)
    for p in nac.POPULATIONS:
        sel = pops == p
        print(f"   {p:22s} n={sel.sum():3d}  {np.round(X[sel].mean(0), 2)}")

The later population claims use the partition, among the claims file's readings, whose centroids best match the five patterns (N4, most frequent partition). This is an assumption: the paper's own labels are not in the data set and its clustering cannot be repeated (the script uses one MATLAB start after rng(1)). Each later claim also shows its result under every partition.

def pop_units(pops, names):
    return [i for i, p in enumerate(pops) if p in names]

prim = partitions[PRIMARY]
print("primary partition:", dict(collections.Counter(prim)))

Firing and waveform characteristics (fig3)¶

Baseline rate: mean firing over the 10 s before onset, all 16 trials. Coefficient of variance, readings of the claims file: (a) inter-spike intervals within the 10-s baselines; (b) the whole session, which in these files is the recorded part of each trial, 20 s before to 33 s after onset (see the data check: nothing is recorded between trials), so intervals across the gaps are left out. The authors' cubeScriptPR25.m pools the intervals within the 20 s before each onset (variant). Peak-valley duration: valley to after-hyperpolarization peak of the mean waveform, counted as mainAnalysis.m counts it ("Get firing characteristics"), in ms at 40 samples per ms. The script then halves it ("HD"), which gives 0.075 to 0.3, half the 0.15 to 0.6 ms of Figure 3C; the unhalved value spans the figure's range, and its boundary 0.3875 falls between the modes only on that scale, so the unhalved value is used. Hartigan's dip with a p-value from 1000 uniform samples, as the authors' HartigansDipSignifTest(x, 1000).

In [20]:
def isi_cv(u, a, b):
    isis = []
    for t in u["onsets"]:
        s = u["spikes"][(u["spikes"] > t + a) & (u["spikes"] <= t + b)]
        isis.append(np.diff(s))
    isi = np.concatenate(isis)
    return isi.std(ddof=1) / isi.mean() if len(isi) > 2 else np.nan

char = pd.DataFrame(dict(
    rate=[nac.window_rate(u["spikes"], u["onsets"], -10, 0).mean() for u in R],
    cv_a=[isi_cv(u, -10, 0) for u in R],
    cv_b=[isi_cv(u, -20, 33) for u in R],
    cv_script=[isi_cv(u, -20, 0) for u in R],
    hd=[nac.peak_valley(u["wave"]) for u in R],
    hd_script=[nac.peak_valley(u["wave"]) / 2 for u in R],
    pv_ms=[nac.peak_valley_ms(u["wave"]) for u in R]))
cube_cv = CUBE.fire[0, 0].CV[:, 0]
cv_check = [abs(cube_cv[cube_index[u["filename"] + u["neuron"]]] - c) < 1e-6 for u, c in zip(R, char.cv_script)]
print(f"cv_script equal to cube.fire.CV: {sum(cv_check)} of {len(R)}")
print(char.describe().round(3))

rng_3 = np.random.default_rng(3001)
rows = []
for key, pops in partitions.items():
    main = pop_units(pops, nac.MAIN3)
    row = dict(partition=key, n=len(main))
    for col in ("rate", "cv_a", "cv_b", "cv_script"):
        x = char[col].to_numpy()[main]
        d, p = nac.dip_test(x[np.isfinite(x)], rng_3, 1000 if key == PRIMARY else 200)
        row[col + " dip"] = d
        row[col + " p"] = p
    rows.append(row)
print(pd.DataFrame(rows).round(3).to_string(index=False))

fig3c-waveform-bimodal¶

Waveform peak-valley duration was bimodal across the 172 units; Threat Inhibited units were mostly wide (bimodal only at an uncorrected p), Danger Excited units unimodal and mostly narrow, and Bidirectional Valence units bimodal with about half narrow and half wide.

Boundary readings: (a) the dashed line of Figure 3C, which halfDuration.m sets at (0.375 + 0.4) / 2 = 0.3875 and which sits at about 0.39 in the figure; (b) the lowest bin between the two modes of the histogram of all main-population units (bins of 0.025).

In [22]:
hd_all = char.hd.to_numpy()
print("peak-valley values (Figure 3C scale):", dict(sorted(collections.Counter(np.round(hd_all, 4)).items())))
print(f"the same in ms at 40 kHz (valley to peak): {np.unique(char.pv_ms).round(3)}")

def trough(x):
    edges = np.arange(0.0125, 0.8, 0.025)  # bins centred on the 0.025-ms sample grid
    h, _ = np.histogram(x, edges)
    lo_mode = np.argmax(np.where(edges[:-1] < 0.35, h, -1))
    hi_mode = np.argmax(np.where(edges[:-1] >= 0.35, h, -1))
    j = lo_mode + np.argmin(h[lo_mode:hi_mode + 1])
    return (edges[j] + edges[j + 1]) / 2

rows = []
for key, pops in partitions.items():
    main = pop_units(pops, nac.MAIN3)
    x = hd_all[main]
    nsim = 1000 if key == PRIMARY else 200
    d_all, p_all = nac.dip_test(x, rng_3, nsim)
    row = dict(partition=key, all_dip=d_all, all_p=p_all, trough=trough(x))
    for p, short in (("Threat Inhibited", "TI"), ("Danger Excited", "DE"), ("Bidirectional Valence", "BV")):
        y = hd_all[pop_units(pops, [p])]
        if len(y) < 5:
            continue
        d, pv = nac.dip_test(y, rng_3, nsim)
        row[short + " n"] = len(y)
        row[short + " dip"] = d
        row[short + " p"] = pv
        row[short + " narrow(a)"] = np.mean(y < nac.HD_BOUNDARY)
        row[short + " narrow(b)"] = np.mean(y < row["trough"])
    rows.append(row)
fig3c_table = pd.DataFrame(rows)
print(fig3c_table.round(3).to_string(index=False))
print("paper: all p = 0; TI dip 0.096 (+-0.0096), p = 0.035; DE dip 0.06 (+-0.006), p = 0.18; BV dip 0.11 (+-0.011), p = 0; BV 45-55% narrow")

Result: reproduced under boundary reading (a), resting on the assumed partition. Under the primary partition the 162 units are bimodal (dip 0.114, p < 0.001). Threat Inhibited units are bimodal (dip 0.125, p = 0.001) and mostly wide (36% narrow at 0.3875). Danger Excited units are unimodal (dip 0.067, p = 0.12) and mostly narrow (82%). Bidirectional Valence units are bimodal (dip 0.119, p < 0.001) with 52% narrow. The dips are within tolerance for Bidirectional Valence (0.11 +- 0.011) and outside it for Threat Inhibited (0.096 +- 0.0096) and Danger Excited (0.06 +- 0.006). With the trough boundary (b, 0.425) the Bidirectional Valence split is 56% narrow, just outside 45 to 55%. The authors' script halves this measure, which would put every unit below the 0.3875 boundary; the data match Figure 3C's scale only unhalved.

Cue firing of the populations (fig4, fig5)¶

Normalized firing as in the opening comment of the claims file; the cue by interval ANOVA over 48 bins of 250 ms from 2 s before onset to offset, uncertainty collapsed; onset is the first 1 s and late cue the last 5 s; studentized bootstrap CIs over units (1000 resamples). The differences are those of the Methods (Population and single-unit firing analyses) and mainAnalysis.m (diff1 = a - b, diff2 = (a + b)/2 - c, diff3 = b - c). "Danger and uncertainty" readings: (a) the mean of the two values; (b) trials pooled, (4 danger + 8 uncertainty) / 12.

In [23]:
def cue_stats(tr, rng):
    """tr: units x 3 cues x 200 bins. ANOVA and window differences."""
    A = nac.rm_anova2(tr[:, :, nac.ANOVA_BINS])
    on = tr[:, :, nac.ONSET].mean(2)
    late = tr[:, :, nac.LATE].mean(2)
    out = dict(n=len(tr), F_cue=A["A"][0], df_cue=f"{A['A'][1]},{A['A'][2]}", p_cue=A["A"][3],
               F_int=A["B"][0], p_int=A["B"][3], F_x=A["AxB"][0], df_x=f"{A['AxB'][1]},{A['AxB'][2]}", p_x=A["AxB"][3])
    for w, v in (("onset", on), ("late", late)):
        d, un, s = v[:, 0], v[:, 1], v[:, 2]
        diffs = {"D-U": d - un, "U-S": un - s, "(D+U)/2-S": (d + un) / 2 - s, "(4D+8U)/12-S": (4 * d + 8 * un) / 12 - s,
                 "D-(U+S)/2": d - (un + s) / 2}
        for k, x in diffs.items():
            m, lo, hi = nac.boot_ci(x, rng)
            out[f"{w} {k}"] = (round(m, 3), round(lo, 3), round(hi, 3))
    return out

def excl(ci):
    return ci[1] > 0 or ci[2] < 0

def pop_traces(pops, p, reading=PRIMARY_NORM):
    return norm[reading][pop_units(pops, [p])]

def run_pop(p, judge, seed):
    """Run cue_stats for population p under every partition (primary normalization; for the
    primary partition also every normalization reading). judge(stats) -> outcome string."""
    rows = []
    for key, pops in partitions.items():
        readings = list(nac.NORM_READINGS) + ["script normalization"] if key == PRIMARY else [PRIMARY_NORM]
        for nr in readings:
            tr = pop_traces(pops, p, nr)
            if len(tr) < 5:
                rows.append(dict(partition=key, normalization=nr, n=len(tr), outcome="too few units"))
                continue
            st = cue_stats(tr, np.random.default_rng(seed))
            rows.append(dict(partition=key, normalization=nr, **st, outcome=judge(st)))
    return pd.DataFrame(rows)

def show(t, cols):
    print(t[["partition", "normalization", "n"] + [c for c in cols if c in t] + ["outcome"]].to_string(index=False))
    tally(t)

Threat Inhibited units decreased firing more to danger and uncertainty than to safety, both at cue onset and during late cue.

def judge_4ab(st):
    ok_cis = []
    for key in ("(D+U)/2-S", "(4D+8U)/12-S"):
        on, la = st["onset " + key], st["late " + key]
        cis = excl(on) and excl(la) and on[0] < 0 and la[0] < 0
        within = abs(on[0] + 0.47) <= 0.15 and abs(la[0] + 0.44) <= 0.16
        ok_cis.append((cis, within))
    anova = st["p_cue"] < 0.05 and st["p_x"] < 0.05
    if any(c and w for c, w in ok_cis) and anova:
        return "reproduced"
    if any(c for c, w in ok_cis):
        return "partially_reproduced"
    return "not_reproduced"

def judge_4cd(st):
    cis = [st["onset D-U"], st["onset U-S"], st["late D-(U+S)/2"]]
    exp = [(0.22, 0.11), (0.21, 0.12), (0.17, 0.10)]
    allx = all(excl(c) and c[0] > 0 for c in cis)
    within = all(abs(c[0] - e) <= t for c, (e, t) in zip(cis, exp))
    if allx and within and st["p_cue"] < 0.05 and st["p_x"] < 0.05:
        return "reproduced"
    return "partially_reproduced" if allx else "not_reproduced"

t4cd = run_pop("Danger Excited", judge_4cd, 4201)

fig4ef-bv-danger-decrease¶

Bidirectional Valence units, the most abundant population (about 47% of cue-responsive units), decreased firing to danger more than to uncertainty and safety, at onset and during late cue.

In [27]:
def judge_4ef(st, share):
    on, la = st["onset D-(U+S)/2"], st["late D-(U+S)/2"]
    allx = excl(on) and excl(la) and on[0] < 0 and la[0] < 0
    within = abs(on[0] + 0.13) <= 0.09 and abs(la[0] + 0.09) <= 0.07 and abs(share - 47) <= 4.7
    if allx and within and st["p_cue"] < 0.05 and st["p_x"] < 0.05:
        return "reproduced"
    return "partially_reproduced" if allx else "not_reproduced"

rows = []
for key, pops in partitions.items():
    share = 100 * np.mean(pops == "Bidirectional Valence")
    readings = list(nac.NORM_READINGS) + ["script normalization"] if key == PRIMARY else [PRIMARY_NORM]
    for nr in readings:
        st = cue_stats(pop_traces(pops, "Bidirectional Valence", nr), np.random.default_rng(4301))
        rows.append(dict(partition=key, normalization=nr, share=share, **st, outcome=judge_4ef(st, share)))

fig4f-bv-safety-variability¶

Bidirectional Valence units varied more in their safety onset firing than in their danger or uncertainty onset firing.

Pitman-Morgan test of paired variances, as the authors' PitmanMorganTest in mainAnalysis.m.

In [28]:
rows = []
for key, pops in partitions.items():
    readings = list(nac.NORM_READINGS) + ["script normalization"] if key == PRIMARY else [PRIMARY_NORM]
    for nr in readings:
        on = pop_traces(pops, "Bidirectional Valence", nr)[:, :, nac.ONSET].mean(2)
        r1 = nac.pitman_morgan(on[:, 2], on[:, 0])
        r2 = nac.pitman_morgan(on[:, 2], on[:, 1])
        ok = r1[2] < 0.05 and r2[2] < 0.05 and r1[0] > 1 and r2[0] > 1
        rows.append(dict(partition=key, normalization=nr, n=len(on), var_ratio_S_D=r1[0], p_S_D=r1[2],
                         var_ratio_S_U=r2[0], p_S_U=r2[2], outcome="reproduced" if ok else "not_reproduced"))
t4f = pd.DataFrame(rows)
show(t4f.round(4), ["var_ratio_S_D", "p_S_D", "var_ratio_S_U", "p_S_U"])
print("paper: p = 0.0087 (safety against danger), 0.0046 (safety against uncertainty), n = 91")

Threat Excited units increased firing more to danger and uncertainty than to safety at onset, but not during late cue.

def judge_5ab(st):
    res = []
    for key in ("(D+U)/2-S", "(4D+8U)/12-S"):
        on, la = st["onset " + key], st["late " + key]
        res.append(st["p_x"] < 0.05 and excl(on) and on[0] > 0 and abs(on[0] - 0.9) <= 0.6 and not excl(la))
    return "reproduced" if any(res) else "not_reproduced"

Threat minus safety firing is above zero at onset (1.38 [0.33, 2.36] with N4) and its late CI contains zero, but the cue by interval interaction is not significant under any normalization reading (p 0.08 to 0.11). The outcome depends on the partition: on the alternative partition (N2, most frequent, smallest cosine 0.77; 7 Threat Excited units) the interaction has p = 0.013, the onset CI is above zero (1.42 [0.44, 2.13]) and the late CI contains zero, so the claim is reproduced there. The CI form, SE and seed do not change the outcome on the primary partition (CI section).

fig5cd-ns-nonselective¶

Non-Selective units showed no main effect of cue and no cue by interval interaction, and their threat and safety firing did not differ at onset or late cue.

def judge_5cd(st):
    # a claim of absence, judged on the paper's tests; reading (a) of "danger and uncertainty"
    ok = st["p_cue"] >= 0.05 and st["p_x"] >= 0.05 and not excl(st["onset (D+U)/2-S"]) and not excl(st["late (D+U)/2-S"])
    return "reproduced" if ok else "not_reproduced"

t5cd = run_pop("Non-Selective", judge_5cd, 5101)

Firing similarity (fig6)¶

Per unit, mean normalized firing to each cue in 12 bins of 1 s from 2 s before onset to offset; Pearson R between the traces of each cue pair. Readings: (a) the mean of four 250-ms normalized bins; (b) 1-s bins normalized in the same way. The authors' mainAnalysis.m correlates the 1-s bins of the script normalization (columns 19 to 30 of fire.z.s1), shown as a variant. Units with a constant trace (no R) are left out and counted. Readings (a) and (b) give the same R for every unit: the two traces differ by an affine map that is the same for the three cues of a unit (the 1-s rate is the mean of four 250-ms rates, and the z-score only shifts and scales), and Pearson R is unchanged by it. They are one reading, computed twice as a check (largest difference printed below).

In [31]:
def similarity(pops, reading, nr=PRIMARY_NORM):
    idx = pop_units(pops, nac.MAIN3)
    out = []
    for i in idx:
        u = R[i]
        if reading == "a":
            tr = norm[nr][i][:, nac.ONSET_BIN - 8: nac.ONSET_BIN + 40].reshape(3, 12, 4).mean(2)
        elif reading == "b":
            kw = nac.NORM_READINGS[nr] if nr in nac.NORM_READINGS else {}
            tr = nac.norm_methods(u, bin_s=1.0, **kw)[0][:, 18:30]
        else:
            z = nac.trial_code(u, 1.0)
            tr = np.array([z[nac.CUE_OF_TRIAL == c].mean(0) for c in range(3)])[:, 18:30]
        rr = [stats.pearsonr(tr[0], tr[1]).statistic if tr[0].std() > 0 and tr[1].std() > 0 else np.nan,
              stats.pearsonr(tr[1], tr[2]).statistic if tr[1].std() > 0 and tr[2].std() > 0 else np.nan,
              stats.pearsonr(tr[0], tr[2]).statistic if tr[0].std() > 0 and tr[2].std() > 0 else np.nan]
        out.append(dict(pop=pops[i], DU=rr[0], US=rr[1], DS=rr[2]))
    return pd.DataFrame(out)

sim = {}
for key, pops in partitions.items():
    for rd in ("a", "b", "script"):
        if key != PRIMARY and rd == "script":
            continue
        sim[(key, rd)] = similarity(pops, rd)

fig6-similarity-anova¶

Temporal firing similarity between cue pairs depended on population and comparison, with a population by comparison interaction.

In [32]:
rows = []
for (key, rd), s in sim.items():
    s = s.dropna()
    groups = [s[s["pop"] == p][["DU", "US", "DS"]].to_numpy() for p in nac.MAIN3]
    A = nac.mixed_anova(groups)
    allp = all(A[k][3] < 0.05 for k in A)
    out = "reproduced" if allp else ("partially_reproduced" if A["group x level"][3] < 0.05 else "not_reproduced")
    rows.append(dict(partition=key, reading=rd, n=len(s), dropped=len(sim[(key, rd)]) - len(s),
                     F_pop=A["group"][0], df_pop=f"{A['group'][1]},{A['group'][2]}", p_pop=A["group"][3],
                     F_comp=A["level"][0], p_comp=A["level"][3], F_x=A["group x level"][0],
                     df_x=f"{A['group x level'][1]},{A['group x level'][2]}", p_x=A["group x level"][3], outcome=out))
t6 = pd.DataFrame(rows)
print(t6.round(4).to_string(index=False))
print("paper: population F(2,169) = 3.34, p = 0.038; comparison F(2,338) = 26.26; interaction F(4,338) = 3.86, p = 0.004")

           for k in partitions)

fig6d-thi-similarity, fig6e-de-similarity, fig6f-bv-no-similarity-difference¶

Paired t tests between comparisons within each population, at the Bonferroni level 0.05 / 9.

In [33]:
rows = []
for (key, rd), s in sim.items():
    s = s.dropna()
    row = dict(partition=key, reading=rd)
    for p, short in (("Threat Inhibited", "TI"), ("Danger Excited", "DE"), ("Bidirectional Valence", "BV")):
        x = s[s["pop"] == p]
        for a, b in (("DU", "US"), ("DU", "DS"), ("US", "DS")):
            t = stats.ttest_rel(x[a], x[b])
            row[f"{short} {a}-{b}"] = (round(t.statistic, 2), float(f"{t.pvalue:.2g}"))
        row[short + " n"] = len(x)
    row["fig6d"] = "reproduced" if all(row[f"TI DU-{c}"][0] > 0 and row[f"TI DU-{c}"][1] < 0.05 / 9 for c in ("US", "DS")) else "not_reproduced"
    row["fig6e"] = "reproduced" if all(row[f"DE DU-{c}"][0] > 0 and row[f"DE DU-{c}"][1] < 0.05 / 9 for c in ("US", "DS")) else "not_reproduced"
    row["fig6f"] = "reproduced" if all(row[f"BV {c}"][1] >= 0.05 / 9 for c in ("DU-US", "DU-DS", "US-DS")) else "not_reproduced"
    rows.append(row)
t6t = pd.DataFrame(rows)

rows = []
for (key, rd), s in sim.items():
    s = s.dropna()
    means = s.groupby("pop")[["DU", "US", "DS"]].mean()
    ti = s[s["pop"] == "Threat Inhibited"].DU
    t1 = stats.ttest_ind(ti, s[s["pop"] == "Danger Excited"].DU)
    t2 = stats.ttest_ind(ti, s[s["pop"] == "Bidirectional Valence"].DU)
    greatest = means.stack().idxmax() == ("Threat Inhibited", "DU")
    ok = [t1.statistic > 0 and t2.statistic > 0 and t1.pvalue < lvl and t2.pvalue < lvl and greatest for lvl in (0.05, 0.05 / 9)]
    rows.append(dict(partition=key, reading=rd, TI_DU=means.loc["Threat Inhibited", "DU"], max_cell=str(means.stack().idxmax()),
                     t_vs_DE=t1.statistic, p_vs_DE=t1.pvalue, t_vs_BV=t2.statistic, p_vs_BV=t2.pvalue,
                     outcome="reproduced" if any(ok) else "not_reproduced"))

fig7-tuning-curves¶

The extreme of the Threat Inhibited tuning curve was at an uncertainty assignment of 0.75, and those of Danger Excited and Bidirectional Valence units at 0.50 ...

Per unit, the 16 trials of its session; the cue regressor 1 for danger, 0 for safety and u for the eight uncertainty trials; linear regression with a constant (Methods, Tuning curve). Per-trial normalization readings (a), (b), (c) of the claims file, in 1-s bins. The regression readings (a) one regression on each trial's 10-s cue mean and (b) the mean of the ten 1-s betas give the same beta, since the beta is linear in the response; both are computed and compared. The authors' cubeScriptPR25.m and mainAnalysis.m regress the script's per-trial normalization on the cue regressor and the trial's suppression ratio together, and probability_1.m averages the betas over the cue's 1-s bins (columns 3 to 12); that is shown as a variant. Per-trial readings (a) and (b) also give the same betas: they differ by a per-trial offset (the trial's 10-s baseline minus its trial type's mean baseline) whose mean is zero within each trial type, and the regressors (the constant and the cue pattern) are constant within a trial type, so the offset is orthogonal to them. They are one reading; the largest difference is printed.

In [35]:
U_LEVELS = (0.0, 0.25, 0.5, 0.75, 1.0)
CUE_1S = slice(20, 30)

def tuning(pops, p, reading, nr=PRIMARY_NORM, with_sr=False):
    betas_a, betas_b = [], []
    kw = nac.NORM_READINGS.get(nr, {})
    for i in pop_units(pops, [p]):
        u = R[i]
        if reading == "script":
            y = nac.trial_code(u, 1.0)
        else:
            _, sd, _ = nac.norm_methods(u, **kw)
            y, _ = nac.trial_norm(u, reading, sd)
        sr, _ = nac.suppression(u, "one")
        ba, bb = [], []
        for lv in U_LEVELS:
            X = np.column_stack([nac.pattern(lv), sr]) if with_sr else nac.pattern(lv)[:, None]
            ba.append(nac.ols_betas(y[:, CUE_1S].mean(1), X)[0])
            bb.append(np.mean(nac.ols_betas(y[:, CUE_1S], X)[0]))
        betas_a.append(ba)
        betas_b.append(bb)
    return np.array(betas_a), np.array(betas_b)
In [36]:
expected_ext = {"Threat Inhibited": 0.75, "Danger Excited": 0.5, "Bidirectional Valence": 0.5}
rows = []
extremes = {}
for key, pops in partitions.items():
    for rd in ("a", "b", "c", "script"):
        if rd == "script" and key != PRIMARY:
            continue
        row = dict(partition=key, normalization=rd)
        hits = 0
        for p in nac.MAIN3:
            ba, bb = tuning(pops, p, rd, with_sr=(rd == "script"))
            mb = np.nanmean(ba, 0)
            ext = U_LEVELS[int(np.nanargmax(np.abs(mb)))]
            row[p + " betas"] = np.round(mb, 3).tolist()
            row[p + " extreme"] = ext
            row[p + " max |a-b|"] = float(np.nanmax(np.abs(ba - bb)))
            hits += ext == expected_ext[p]
            extremes[(key, rd, p)] = ext
        row["matches"] = hits
        row["outcome"] = "reproduced" if hits == 3 else ("partially_reproduced" if hits == 2 else "not_reproduced")
        rows.append(row)
t7 = pd.DataFrame(rows)
print(t7.drop(columns=[c for c in t7 if c.endswith("|a-b|")]).to_string(index=False))
print(f"largest difference between regression readings (a) and (b): {t7[[c for c in t7 if c.endswith('|a-b|')]].to_numpy().max():.2e}")
d_rd = max(np.nanmax(np.abs(np.array(t7[(t7.partition == k) & (t7.normalization == "a")][p + " betas"].iloc[0])
                              - np.array(t7[(t7.partition == k) & (t7.normalization == "b")][p + " betas"].iloc[0])))
           for k in partitions for p in nac.MAIN3)
print(f"largest difference in mean beta between per-trial readings (a) and (b): {d_rd:.1e} (betas rounded to 0.001)")

Fear output and exaggerated threat (fig8)¶

Per unit and 1-s bin (2 s before onset to offset, 12 bins), per-trial normalized firing over the 16 trials regressed on the trial's suppression ratio (fear output) and the cue pattern with the population's uncertainty value (0.75, 0.50, 0.50, as the criterion gives), with a constant. Readings: (a) one model with both regressors; (b) a separate model for each. Per-trial normalization readings (a), (b), (c); 0/0 suppression ratios left out (trial dropped) or set to 0, and set to 1 as the scripts do (variant). Early cue is the first 5 s, late cue the last 5 s. Units whose suppression ratio does not vary are left out. Repeated-measures ANOVA on beta, regressor by bin.

In [37]:
BINS12 = slice(18, 30)

def fig8(pops, p, uval, norm_reading, model, nan_reading, nr=PRIMARY_NORM):
    kw = nac.NORM_READINGS.get(nr, {})
    B = []
    dropped = 0
    for i in pop_units(pops, [p]):
        u = R[i]
        if norm_reading == "script":
            y = nac.trial_code(u, 1.0)
        else:
            _, sd, _ = nac.norm_methods(u, **kw)
            y, _ = nac.trial_norm(u, norm_reading, sd)
        y = y[:, BINS12]
        sr, _ = nac.suppression(u, nan_reading)
        keep = np.isfinite(sr)
        if keep.sum() < 4 or np.nanstd(sr[keep]) == 0 or not np.all(np.isfinite(y)):
            dropped += 1
            continue
        pat = nac.pattern(uval)
        if model == "joint":
            b = nac.ols_betas(y[keep], np.column_stack([sr[keep], pat[keep]]))
            fo, et = b[0], b[1]
        else:
            fo = nac.ols_betas(y[keep], sr[keep][:, None])[0]
            et = nac.ols_betas(y[keep], pat[keep][:, None])[0]
        if not (np.all(np.isfinite(fo)) and np.all(np.isfinite(et))):
            dropped += 1
            continue
        B.append(np.stack([fo, et]))
    return np.array(B), dropped

def fig8_stats(B, rng):
    A = nac.rm_anova2(B)
    early, late = slice(2, 7), slice(7, 12)
    out = dict(n=len(B), F_reg=A["A"][0], df_reg=f"{A['A'][1]},{A['A'][2]}", p_reg=A["A"][3],
               F_x=A["AxB"][0], df_x=f"{A['AxB'][1]},{A['AxB'][2]}", p_x=A["AxB"][3])
    for w, sl in (("early", early), ("late", late)):
        fo, et = B[:, 0, sl].mean(1), B[:, 1, sl].mean(1)
        for k, x in (("ET", et), ("FO", fo), ("ET-FO", et - fo)):
            m, lo, hi = nac.boot_ci(x, rng)
            out[f"{w} {k}"] = (round(m, 3), round(lo, 3), round(hi, 3))
    return out

def run_fig8(p, uval, judge, seed):
    rows = []
    for key, pops in partitions.items():
        for nrd in ("a", "b", "c", "script"):
            for model in ("joint", "separate"):
                for nan_r in ("drop", "zero", "one"):
                    if key != PRIMARY and not (nrd == "a" and model == "joint" and nan_r == "drop"):
                        continue
                    if nrd == "script" and not (model == "joint" and nan_r == "one"):
                        continue
                    B, dropped = fig8(pops, p, uval, nrd, model, nan_r)
                    if len(B) < 3:
                        rows.append(dict(partition=key, norm=nrd, model=model, zero_over_zero=nan_r, n=len(B), outcome="too few units"))
                        continue
                    st = fig8_stats(B, np.random.default_rng(seed))
                    rows.append(dict(partition=key, norm=nrd, model=model, zero_over_zero=nan_r, dropped=dropped, **st, outcome=judge(st)))
    t = pd.DataFrame(rows)
    return t

def show8(t, cols):
    print(t[["partition", "norm", "model", "zero_over_zero", "n", "dropped"] + cols + ["outcome"]].to_string(index=False))
    claims_readings = t[t.norm != "script"]
    tally(claims_readings, "claims-file readings")

Threat Inhibited firing signaled exaggerated threat and not fear output; exaggerated threat betas were below zero at early and late cue, and differed from fear output betas at early but not late cue.

def judge_8ab(st):
    e, l, fe, fl, de, dl = (st[k] for k in ("early ET", "late ET", "early FO", "late FO", "early ET-FO", "late ET-FO"))
    core = excl(e) and excl(l) and e[0] < 0 and l[0] < 0 and not excl(fe) and not excl(fl)
    full = (core and st["p_x"] < 0.05 and excl(de) and not excl(dl) and abs(e[0] + 0.97) <= 0.41
            and abs(l[0] + 0.47) <= 0.39 and abs(de[0] + 0.92) <= 0.6)
    return "reproduced" if full else ("partially_reproduced" if core else "not_reproduced")

t8ab = run_fig8("Threat Inhibited", 0.75, judge_8ab, 8001)
show8(t8ab, ["F_x", "df_x", "p_x", "early ET", "late ET", "early FO", "late FO", "early ET-FO", "late ET-FO"])

def judge_8cd(st):
    e, l, fe, fl, de, dl = (st[k] for k in ("early ET", "late ET", "early FO", "late FO", "early ET-FO", "late ET-FO"))
    core = excl(e) and excl(l) and e[0] > 0 and l[0] > 0 and not excl(fe) and not excl(fl)
    full = (core and st["p_reg"] < 0.05 and excl(de) and de[0] > 0 and not excl(dl) and abs(e[0] - 0.43) <= 0.18
            and abs(l[0] - 0.3) <= 0.21 and abs(de[0] - 0.39) <= 0.29)
    return "reproduced" if full else ("partially_reproduced" if core else "not_reproduced")

fig8ef-bv-exaggerated-threat¶

Bidirectional Valence firing signaled exaggerated threat and not fear output; early exaggerated threat betas were below zero while fear output betas were not, and the two differed at early but not late cue.

In [40]:
def judge_8ef(st):
    e, fe, de, dl = (st[k] for k in ("early ET", "early FO", "early ET-FO", "late ET-FO"))
    full = (st["p_reg"] < 0.05 and st["p_x"] < 0.05 and excl(e) and e[0] < 0 and excl(de) and de[0] < 0
            and abs(e[0] + 0.31) <= 0.14 and abs(de[0] + 0.39) <= 0.23 and not excl(fe) and not excl(dl))
    core = excl(e) and e[0] < 0 and not excl(fe)
    return "reproduced" if full else ("partially_reproduced" if core else "not_reproduced")

The script's procedure (variant) meets every part (-0.30 [-0.47, -0.14]).

Reward and nose poke cessation firing (fig9, fig10)¶

Pellet feeder advances (EVT02) and nose pokes followed by at least 2 s without a poke, in the ITI. ITI readings of the claims file: (a) the event's 4-s window outside 20 s before onset to 20 s after offset; (b) the window overlapping no cue and no shock. Normalization readings: (i) the event-averaged trace minus its 1-s pre-event mean, divided by the unit's SD from the cue normalization (the primary normalization, N4); (ii) the same trace z-scored over its 16 bins. The authors' script (cubeScriptPR25.m) takes events 18 to 2 s before onset or 14 to 28 s after it (pellets), and 18 to 2 s before onset (cessation), and z-scores per event; shown as a variant, alone and with the two normalizations of the claims file. Units with no event are left out. Pre is the 250-ms bin before the event, post the first bin after it. The claims-file readings subtract the mean of the 1 s before the event (bins 5 to 8 of 16), as the Methods say ("mean of 1 s before reward delivery"); the script subtracts its bins 1 to 4, 2 to 1 s before the event (cubeScriptPR25.m lines 559 and 674, printed in the data check), and the script variant keeps that.

The files hold nothing from about 31 to 33.5 s after one onset to 20 s before the next (data check). An event counts only when its 4-s window lies inside a recorded segment, (onset - 20, onset + 30] s, and a cessation only when its 2 s without a poke lie inside the segment; otherwise a poke just before a pause in the recording would count as a cessation, and a window would run into time with no recording. ITI reading (a) asks for a window outside (onset - 20, onset + 30], so it can hold no recorded event: it cannot be tested. The counts dropped by this rule are printed. The ITI window and the event normalization are details the Methods leave open and the authors' script settles, so the script's reading is run as a reading taken from the paper's code (author, step 1.6). The cue firing used in the correlations is the claims file's normalization.

sd_primary = np.array([nac.norm_methods(u, **nac.NORM_READINGS[PRIMARY_NORM])[1] for u in R])
# ITI reading (a, b, or the script's windows) and normalization (sd, z16, or the script's per-event z-score)
EVENT_READINGS = [("a", "sd"), ("a", "z16"), ("b", "sd"), ("b", "z16"), ("script", "sd"), ("script", "z16"), ("script", "code")]
ALT_READING = ("b", "sd")  # the reading shown for partitions other than the primary one

def event_traces(kind):
    out = {}
    for itr, nm in EVENT_READINGS:
        tr, n_ev = [], []
        for i, u in enumerate(R):
            if kind == "pellet":
                ev = nac.iti_events_code(u["pellet"], u) if itr == "script" else nac.iti_events(u["pellet"], u, itr)
            else:
                ev = nac.cessations(u, "code" if itr == "script" else itr)
            n_ev.append(len(ev))
            tr.append(nac.event_trace(u, ev, nm, sd_primary[i]))
        out[(itr, nm)] = (np.array(tr), np.array(n_ev))
    return out

reward = event_traces("pellet")
cess = event_traces("cessation")
for name, d in (("reward", reward), ("cessation", cess)):
    for k, (tr, n_ev) in d.items():
        print(f"{name} {k}: units with events {np.sum(np.isfinite(tr[:, 0]))} of {len(tr)}, events per unit median {np.median(n_ev):.0f} (range {n_ev.min()} to {n_ev.max()})")

print("events whose window runs past a recorded segment (or cessations whose 2 s do), dropped:")
for rd in ("a", "b"):
    n_all = sum(len(nac.iti_events(u["pellet"], u, rd, require_recorded=False)) for u in R)
    n_rec = sum(len(nac.iti_events(u["pellet"], u, rd)) for u in R)
    c_all = sum(len(nac.cessations(u, rd, require_recorded=False)) for u in R)
    c_rec = sum(len(nac.cessations(u, rd)) for u in R)
    print(f"  ITI reading ({rd}): rewards {n_all - n_rec} of {n_all} dropped, cessations {c_all - c_rec} of {c_all} dropped")
print("the script's windows (18 to 2 s before onset, 14 to 28 s after) lie inside the segments: nothing dropped")

def has_events(tr):
    return bool(np.isfinite(tr).any())

def event_anova(tr, pops):
    groups = []
    for p in nac.MAIN3:
        x = tr[pop_units(pops, [p])]
        groups.append(x[np.all(np.isfinite(x), 1)])
    A = nac.mixed_anova(groups)
    per = {p: nac.rm_anova1(g) for p, g in zip(nac.MAIN3, groups)}
    return A, per, groups

rows = []
for key, pops in partitions.items():

        if key != PRIMARY and k != ALT_READING:

fig9bcd-reward-pre-post¶

def pre_post(tr, pops, rng):
    out = {}
    for p in nac.MAIN3:
        x = tr[pop_units(pops, [p])]
        x = x[np.all(np.isfinite(x), 1)]
        if len(x) < 3:
            for k in ("pre", "post", "post-pre"):
                out[f"{p[:2]} {k}"] = (np.nan, np.nan, np.nan)
            continue
        for k, v in (("pre", x[:, 7]), ("post", x[:, 8]), ("post-pre", x[:, 8] - x[:, 7])):
            m, lo, hi = nac.boot_ci(v, rng)
            out[f"{p[:2]} {k}"] = (round(m, 3), round(lo, 3), round(hi, 3))
    return out

def judge_9b(o):
    bv = (not excl(o["Bi pre"]) and excl(o["Bi post"]) and o["Bi post"][0] > 0 and excl(o["Bi post-pre"])
          and o["Bi post-pre"][0] > 0 and abs(o["Bi post"][0] - 0.47) <= 0.15 and abs(o["Bi post-pre"][0] - 0.42) <= 0.16)
    others = not any(excl(o[f"{p} {w}"]) for p in ("Th", "Da") for w in ("pre", "post", "post-pre"))
    return "reproduced" if bv and others else ("partially_reproduced" if bv else "not_reproduced")

rows = []
for key, pops in partitions.items():
    for k, (tr, _) in reward.items():
        if key != PRIMARY and k != ALT_READING:
            continue
        if not has_events(tr):
            rows.append(dict(partition=key, reading=k, n=0, outcome="no events"))
            continue
        o = pre_post(tr, pops, np.random.default_rng(9101))
        if any(np.isnan(v[0]) for v in o.values()):
            rows.append(dict(partition=key, reading=k, **o, outcome="too few units"))
            continue
        rows.append(dict(partition=key, reading=k, **o, outcome=judge_9b(o)))
t9b = pd.DataFrame(rows)

outcomes, fitting partitions: {'not_reproduced': 6, 'no events': 2, 'partially_reproduced': 1} | primary: {'no events': 2, 'not_reproduced': 4, 'partially_reproduced': 1} | alternative: {'not_reproduced': 1}

Result: not reproduced, under the assumed partition. Under ITI reading (b), the only one with events, the Bidirectional Valence part fails with both normalizations: with (i) its pre-reward firing is above zero (0.18 [0.02, 0.34]) and its post-reward mean (1.43) is outside 0.47 +- 0.15; with (ii) the post-reward mean (0.78) and the rise (0.66) are outside their tolerances. Threat Inhibited post-reward firing and its rise exclude zero under both (post 0.67 [0.12, 1.35] and 0.58 [0.04, 1.10]; rise 0.71 [0.15, 1.37] and 0.90 [0.32, 1.45]). The script's ITI windows with the claims file's normalizations give the same pattern (Bidirectional Valence post 1.43 and 0.78). Only the script's full procedure (its windows, a baseline of 2 to 1 s before the event and a per-event z-score, which the Methods do not describe) meets the Bidirectional Valence part (pre 0.07 [-0.01, 0.17], post 0.46 [0.31, 0.68], post minus pre 0.39 [0.20, 0.61]), with Threat Inhibited post-reward CIs excluding zero (0.24 [0.03, 0.49]); that variant would be partially reproduced. The CI form, SE and seed do not change these outcomes (CI section).

fig9efg-reward-cue-correlations¶

Bidirectional Valence reward onset firing correlated positively with safety onset firing and negatively with danger firing, and the two correlations differed; ...

Reward onset firing is the post bin of the reward trace; safety onset is the first 1 s and danger the whole 10-s cue of the normalized cue traces (the primary normalization N4; the authors' cue0onset and cue100entire). Comparison readings: (a) Fisher r-to-z for independent correlations; (b) Steiger's test for dependent correlations sharing a variable.

In [44]:
cue_on_s = norm[PRIMARY_NORM][:, 2, nac.ONSET].mean(1)
cue_whole_d = norm[PRIMARY_NORM][:, 0, nac.WHOLE_CUE].mean(1)

def reward_corr(tr, pops):
    out = {}
    for p in nac.MAIN3:
        idx = np.array(pop_units(pops, [p]))
        ok = np.isfinite(tr[idx, 8])
        rw, s, d = tr[idx, 8][ok], cue_on_s[idx][ok], cue_whole_d[idx][ok]
        n = len(rw)
        if n < 4:
            out[p] = dict(n=n, r_safety=np.nan, R2_safety=np.nan, p_safety=np.nan, r_danger=np.nan, R2_danger=np.nan,
                          p_danger=np.nan, fisher_p=np.nan, steiger_p=np.nan)
            continue
        rs, rd, rsd = stats.pearsonr(rw, s), stats.pearsonr(rw, d), stats.pearsonr(s, d).statistic
        out[p] = dict(n=n, r_safety=rs.statistic, R2_safety=rs.statistic**2, p_safety=rs.pvalue, r_danger=rd.statistic,
                      R2_danger=rd.statistic**2, p_danger=rd.pvalue,
                      fisher_p=nac.fisher_independent(rs.statistic, n, rd.statistic, n)[1],
                      steiger_p=nac.steiger_dependent(rs.statistic, rd.statistic, rsd, n)[1])
    return out

rows = []
for key, pops in partitions.items():
    for k, (tr, _) in reward.items():
        if key != PRIMARY and k != ALT_READING:
            continue
        if not has_events(tr):
            rows.append(dict(partition=key, reading=k, n=0, outcome="no events"))
            continue
        o = reward_corr(tr, pops)
        if any(o[p]["n"] < 4 for p in nac.MAIN3):
            for p in nac.MAIN3:
                rows.append(dict(partition=key, reading=k, pop=p, **o[p], outcome="too few units"))
            continue
        res = []
        for cmp in ("fisher_p", "steiger_p"):
            b, ti, de = o["Bidirectional Valence"], o["Threat Inhibited"], o["Danger Excited"]
            bv = b["r_safety"] > 0 and b["p_safety"] < 0.05 and b["r_danger"] < 0 and b["p_danger"] < 0.05 and b[cmp] < 0.05
            ti_ok = ti[cmp] >= 0.05
            rest = ti["p_safety"] >= 0.05 and de["p_safety"] >= 0.05 and de["p_danger"] >= 0.05 and de[cmp] < 0.05
            res.append("reproduced" if bv and ti_ok and rest else ("partially_reproduced" if bv and ti_ok or bv else "not_reproduced"))
        best = "reproduced" if "reproduced" in res else ("partially_reproduced" if "partially_reproduced" in res else "not_reproduced")
        for p in nac.MAIN3:
            rows.append(dict(partition=key, reading=k, pop=p, **o[p], outcome=best))
t9e = pd.DataFrame(rows)
print(t9e.round(4).to_string(index=False))

paper: BV R2 0.08 (p = 0.006) safety, 0.12 (p = 8.4e-4) danger, Z = -3.96; TI 0.07 (p = 0.18), 0.15 (p = 0.05), Z = -0.50; DE 0.06 (p = 0.07), 0.06 (p = 0.07), Z = -2.10

Result: partially reproduced, resting on the assumed partition. Under ITI reading (b) with either normalization, Bidirectional Valence reward onset firing correlates positively with safety onset firing (R2 = 0.055, p = 0.028 with (i); 0.053, p = 0.030 with (ii)) and negatively with danger firing (R2 = 0.123, p = 0.0007; 0.062, p = 0.019), the two correlations differ (Fisher and Steiger p of 0.0023 or less), and Threat Inhibited shows no difference between its correlations (p 0.46 to 0.60). The Danger Excited pattern does not hold: its danger correlation is significant (p 0.012 and 0.020). The script's windows, alone or with its normalization, give the same outcome; with the script's full procedure the R2 values (0.088 and 0.109) are close to the paper's 0.08 and 0.12, and under reading (b) with (i) they are 0.055 and 0.123.

Note on the criterion's partial outcome: the claims file's text reads "partially reproduced when the Bidirectional Valence part holds and the Threat Inhibited or Danger Excited pattern of the statement does not". The table's outcome column gives "reproduced" only when the Bidirectional Valence part, the Threat Inhibited test of no difference, and the rest of the pattern all hold.

fig9hij-danger-safety-correlations¶

Danger firing correlated negatively with safety onset firing in Danger Excited and Bidirectional Valence units but not in Threat Inhibited units.

In [45]:
rows = []
for key, pops in partitions.items():
    for nr in (list(nac.NORM_READINGS) + ["script normalization"] if key == PRIMARY else [PRIMARY_NORM]):
        s_on = norm[nr][:, 2, nac.ONSET].mean(1)
        d_wh = norm[nr][:, 0, nac.WHOLE_CUE].mean(1)
        row = dict(partition=key, normalization=nr)
        for p in nac.MAIN3:
            idx = pop_units(pops, [p])
            r = stats.pearsonr(d_wh[idx], s_on[idx])
            row[f"{p[:2]} n"] = len(idx)
            row[f"{p[:2]} r"] = r.statistic
            row[f"{p[:2]} R2"] = r.statistic ** 2
            row[f"{p[:2]} p"] = r.pvalue
        ok = row["Da r"] < 0 and row["Da p"] < 0.05 and row["Bi r"] < 0 and row["Bi p"] < 0.05 and row["Th p"] >= 0.05
        row["outcome"] = "reproduced" if ok else "not_reproduced"
        rows.append(row)
t9h = pd.DataFrame(rows)
print(t9h.round(4).to_string(index=False))
print("paper: TI R2 0.06, p = 0.24; DE R2 0.25 (+-0.025), p = 1.2e-4; BV R2 0.06 (+-0.006), p = 0.015")

Result: not reproduced, resting on the assumed partition. With the claims file's normalization, Danger Excited danger firing correlates negatively with safety onset firing (R2 0.11 to 0.14, p 0.01 to 0.03, below the paper's 0.25), but Bidirectional Valence firing does not (R2 0.008 to 0.011, p 0.33 to 0.40); Threat Inhibited shows no correlation (p 0.33 to 0.64). With the script normalization (variant) the pattern holds (DE R2 0.21, p = 0.001; BV R2 0.062, p = 0.019; TI p = 0.44).

fig10a-poke-cessation-anova¶

The three populations showed minimal firing changes around nose poke cessation in the ITI; there was no main effect of interval, and a population by interval interaction driven by modest Bidirectional Valence increases.

In [46]:
rows = []
for key, pops in partitions.items():
    for k, (tr, _) in cess.items():
        if key != PRIMARY and k != ALT_READING:
            continue
        if not has_events(tr):
            rows.append(dict(partition=key, reading=k, n=0, outcome="no events"))
            continue
        A, per, groups = event_anova(tr, pops)
        ok = A["level"][3] >= 0.05 and A["group x level"][3] < 0.05
        rows.append(dict(partition=key, reading=k, n=sum(len(g) for g in groups),
                         F_bin=A["level"][0], df_bin=f"{A['level'][1]},{A['level'][2]}", p_bin=A["level"][3],
                         F_x=A["group x level"][0], df_x=f"{A['group x level'][1]},{A['group x level'][2]}", p_x=A["group x level"][3],
                         outcome="reproduced" if ok else "not_reproduced"))
t10a = pd.DataFrame(rows)
print(t10a.round(4).to_string(index=False))
print("paper: interval F(15,2535) = 1.58, p = 0.071; interaction F(30,2535) = 2.18, p = 2.35e-4")

Result: reproduced, resting on the assumed partition. ITI reading (a) holds no cessation inside a recorded segment (306 of 306 dropped) and cannot be tested. Under ITI reading (b) there is no effect of interval (F(15,2385) = 1.21, p = 0.26 with (i); 1.43, p = 0.12 with (ii)) and a population by interval interaction (F(30,2385) = 3.07 and 2.47, p < 1e-4). The script's windows give the same with each normalization (interval p 0.26 to 0.49, interaction p 0.004 to 0.010). Under reading (b), 2737 of 15363 candidate cessations were dropped because their 2 s without a poke ran into a pause in the recording.

fig10bcd-poke-cessation-pre-post¶

Pre and post nose poke cessation firing differed neither from zero nor from each other in Threat Inhibited and Danger Excited units; in Bidirectional Valence units both exceeded zero but did not differ.

In [47]:
def judge_10b(o):
    others = not any(excl(o[f"{p} {w}"]) for p in ("Th", "Da") for w in ("pre", "post", "post-pre"))
    bv = excl(o["Bi pre"]) and o["Bi pre"][0] > 0 and excl(o["Bi post"]) and o["Bi post"][0] > 0 and not excl(o["Bi post-pre"])
    return "reproduced" if others and bv else "not_reproduced"

rows = []
for key, pops in partitions.items():
    for k, (tr, _) in cess.items():
        if key != PRIMARY and k != ALT_READING:
            continue
        if not has_events(tr):
            rows.append(dict(partition=key, reading=k, n=0, outcome="no events"))
            continue
        o = pre_post(tr, pops, np.random.default_rng(10101))
        if any(np.isnan(v[0]) for v in o.values()):
            rows.append(dict(partition=key, reading=k, **o, outcome="too few units"))
            continue
        rows.append(dict(partition=key, reading=k, **o, outcome=judge_10b(o)))
t10b = pd.DataFrame(rows)
print(t10b.to_string(index=False))

rows = []
for key, pops in partitions.items():
    for k, (tr, _) in cess.items():
        if key != PRIMARY and k != ALT_READING:
            continue
        if not has_events(tr):
            rows.append(dict(partition=key, reading=k, n=0, outcome="no events"))
            continue
        o = reward_corr(tr, pops)
        if any(o[p]["n"] < 4 for p in nac.MAIN3):
            for p in nac.MAIN3:
                rows.append(dict(partition=key, reading=k, pop=p, n=o[p]["n"], outcome="too few units"))
            continue
        ps = [o[p][c] for p in nac.MAIN3 for c in ("p_safety", "p_danger")]
        for p in nac.MAIN3:
            rows.append(dict(partition=key, reading=k, pop=p, n=o[p]["n"], R2_safety=o[p]["R2_safety"], p_safety=o[p]["p_safety"],
                             R2_danger=o[p]["R2_danger"], p_danger=o[p]["p_danger"],
                             outcome="reproduced" if min(ps) >= 0.05 else "not_reproduced"))
t10e = pd.DataFrame(rows)

for claim, t in tables.items():
    def outs(key, t=t):
        s = t[t.partition == key]
        if "normalization" in s and key == PRIMARY:
            s = s[s.normalization != "script normalization"]
        return dict(sorted(collections.Counter(s.outcome).items()))
    rows.append(dict(claim=claim, primary=outs(PRIMARY), alternative=outs(ALT)))

Primary and alternative partition¶

The primary partition is the claims-file partition whose centroids best match the five patterns (smallest cosine 0.79, N4 most frequent); the next best (0.77, N2 most frequent) is nearly as good. The outcomes of every population claim on the two, read from the tables above (for each, the rows of that partition: under every normalization for the primary partition, under the primary normalization for the alternative).

In [50]:
CI_CONFIGS = [(False, "analytic"), (True, "analytic"), (False, "boot"), (True, "boot")]
CI_SEEDS = range(5)

def ci_robustness(claim, reading, run):
    """run(rng) -> outcome, repeated under each interval form, SE and seed."""
    rows = []
    for mir, se in CI_CONFIGS:
        nac.CI_MIRRORED, nac.CI_SE = mir, se
        try:
            outs = [run(np.random.default_rng(70000 + s)) for s in CI_SEEDS]
        finally:
            nac.CI_MIRRORED, nac.CI_SE = False, "analytic"
        rows.append(dict(claim=claim, reading=reading, interval="non-reflected" if mir else "reflected", se=se,
                         outcomes=dict(sorted(collections.Counter(outs).items()))))
    return rows

def judge_1g(o):
    cis = o["D-U excl0"] and o["U-S excl0"] and o["D-S excl0"]
    within = abs(o["D-U"] - 0.21) <= 0.05 and abs(o["U-S"] - 0.47) <= 0.12 and abs(o["D-S"] - 0.69) <= 0.19
    if o["p"] < 0.05 and cis:
        return "reproduced" if within else "partially_reproduced"
    return "not_reproduced"

t_start = time.time()
ci_rows = []
for sl, S in session_sets.items():

        ci_rows += ci_robustness("fig1g-discrimination", f"{sl[:3]} sessions, 0/0 {nl}", lambda rng: judge_1g(fig1g(beh, rng)))

CI form, standard error and seed¶

Every CI-based judgement above uses the standard (reflected) studentized interval with the analytic standard error in each resample and one seed per claim. The authors call bootci(1000, {@mean, x}, 'type', 'student') (printed in the data check), which estimates the standard error by an inner bootstrap, and the paper's printed Figure 1G intervals match the bootstrap-t quantiles added without reflection (fig1g-discrimination). Here each CI-based claim is judged again on the primary partition under the two interval forms, the two standard errors (analytic, and an inner bootstrap of 100 resamples as bootci uses) and five seeds, under the readings its outcome rests on. The counts are the outcomes over the five seeds.

Summary¶

A failure to reproduce here may be a failure of this attempt, not of the paper: see the reasoning and the differences from the paper's method.

fig8cd-de-exaggerated-threat partially reproduced (assumed partition)
fig8ef-bv-exaggerated-threat partially reproduced (assumed partition)
fig9a-reward-firing not reproduced (assumed partition)
fig9bcd-reward-pre-post not reproduced (assumed partition)
fig9efg-reward-cue-correlations partially reproduced (assumed partition)
fig9hij-danger-safety-correlations not reproduced (assumed partition)
fig10a-poke-cessation-anova reproduced (assumed partition)
fig10bcd-poke-cessation-pre-post not reproduced (assumed partition)
fig10efg-poke-cessation-cue-unrelated not reproduced (assumed partition)

How the analysis differs from the paper's. The populations are not the paper's: its labels are not deposited, and its k-means cannot be repeated (one MATLAB start after rng(1)), so the population claims rest on a reproduced partition chosen among the claims file's readings. The cue firing normalization follows the Methods; the authors' script normalizes differently (per trial, minus a floored 2-s baseline, z-scored over all trials), and that variant brings several effect sizes to the paper's values (fig4ab, fig4cd, fig8ef, and the Bidirectional Valence R2 of fig9hij; its Danger Excited R2, 0.212, stays outside 0.25 +- 0.025). The cue-responsive set is the script's (floored rates), the only procedure that gives 193 units. The primary partition is the one of the claims file's readings whose centroids best match the five described patterns; the next best (N2, most frequent) is nearly as good, and the two give different outcomes for fig5ab-the-onset and for the Threat Inhibited extreme of fig7-tuning-curves (section "Primary and alternative partition"). ITI events count only when their 4-s window lies inside a recorded segment, and a cessation only when its 2 s without a poke do; this leaves ITI reading (a) with no event, so the reward and cessation claims rest on reading (b), with the script's windows as a variant. Their baseline is the 1 s before the event, as the Methods say; the script uses 2 to 1 s before. The bootstrap intervals are standard studentized intervals; the paper's printed intervals match a form that does not reflect the bootstrap-t quantiles. Judged again under both forms, the bootstrap SE of bootci and five seeds (section "CI form"), fig1g-discrimination is reproduced under every reading without reflection and under one reading, in some seeds, with it. For fig8ab and fig8cd some readings move between outcomes across seeds and forms; the readings fig8ab's outcome rests on hold in every case, and fig8cd stays partially reproduced under two of the three 0/0 readings of per-trial reading (a). The other CI-based claims keep their outcomes in every seed, form and SE.

What was found about the data set. It holds all 368 units and 7 rats, from 90 sessions where the paper reports 95. The pre-surgery sessions and the paper's population labels are not in it. The data description does not say which event channel is which (the script does), nor that the recording is paused between trials, so that the files hold only 20 s before to about 33 s after each onset; it describes poke.base as a 2-s rate where the values are 20-s rates. The script's waveform measure is half the scale of Figure 3C.
